# Audit ledger — issue #155 (M12.17 connector dynamic path and connector/replay contracts)

Audit record for the completion workflow (`CLAUDE.md`, amended 2026-08-12 / -08-14; standing
rules in `docs/architecture/COMPLETION_WORKFLOW_RULES.md`).

Plan of record: `.codex/plans/issue-155.md` (the attested architect plan, plus the folded
2026-08-25 addendum and the corrections that override it). The architect gate attested it at sha256
`d60496a6fb2083f9c371deae8dc84009051aa44551204fe157aa45b3fee00f0d`; attestation
`.codex/plans/issue-155.attest.json`. On 2026-08-27 the owner AMENDED it, appending a dated section
adopting the maintained evidence archiver as in-scope, which moves the file to sha256
`3020c7c2d40bc23a2c8b3b10d6d2155b09b6b4bf42f41f416bf30e666df0eb2f`. Both digests are recorded so the
attested bytes stay identifiable: the attestation covers the plan as it stood BEFORE the amendment,
and the amendment adds scope without revoking a contract, gate or acceptance criterion. The landing strategy this ledger executes lives at
`docs/plans/issue-155-strategy.md` (local-only, like every plan file here).

## Slice map (six governed slices, ONE open issue, ONE ledger, ONE archive)

The issue is landed in six separately-gated slices. Each gets its own Stage-1 step-0 baseline
block below, its own roster windows, its own composite wave gate and its own closing protocol;
#155 stays OPEN until the last one. A finding whose fix belongs to a later slice's unit is
deferred `blocked-by-mechanism` naming that slice, never silently carried.

| Slice | Scope | Kind | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A | canonical dynamic-path binding on both connector roles, its lineage rule, the published family capability, and the explicit process-source replay policy | behaviour-affecting | **CLOSED CLEAN, landed on the integration branch at `6d2205a` 2026-08-27** |
| B | the packaged replay-evidence registry, identifier grammar, both digest algorithms, the derived audit report, the execution-connector monitor action | src-changing, narrowly reachable | IN PROGRESS — baseline `6d2205a` |
| C | the trusted connector-resolution snapshot and the blank-path cross-check | behaviour-affecting | not started |
| D | per-call replay grants, the shared reference grammar, candidate discovery, the account-independent semantic revision | mostly unreachable in production | not started |
| E | apply-boundary identity rechecks and the evidence attestation tuple | mutation accounting | not started |
| F | evidence ingestion and production enablement — the only slice that can close #155 | evidence-gated | not started |

Diagnostic identifiers a later slice introduces are described here in prose until the slice that
registers them lands; the ledger names a code only once the taxonomy carries it.

## Artifact trust boundary

The slice CREATES AND OWNS: the canonical path binding and its semantic mirrors, the family
capability authority and its published projection, the replay-evidence package and its derived
report, the resolution snapshot, the grant symbols, the attestation tuple, the issue-155 goldens
and fixtures (regression pins, never their own oracle), this ledger and its evidence archive, and
its own entries in the wave-gate test module. It CONSUMES, and never re-authors: the legacy
process-flow builder and shape renderer (the parity oracle), the Boomi platform readbacks and
execution records, the sandbox counterparty, and the committed wave-gate manifests. A finding that
only hardens a self-owned artifact gets a boundary verdict at reconciliation instead of a fix batch
by default.

### Pre-committed stopping rule for the evidence archiver (recorded BEFORE the round it governs)

Rounds 5, 6 and 7 of the Stage-2 loop all landed on one artifact — the archiver this slice had to
write because no producer for the archive index existed — and nothing they found reaches a served
surface. That artifact is FINITE; the space of malformed attestations, mis-supplied paths and
foreign index shapes it could be asked to survive is UNBOUNDED. This repository has twice recorded
what that costs: a finite artifact bound to an unbounded space does not converge, and the rounds
keep finding correct defects until someone states a rule. Stated here before round 8 opens, so it
is a rule and not a rationalisation of whatever that round returns.

From round 8, each archiver finding is judged into exactly one bucket at reconciliation:

1. It can damage something this slice does not own — another issue's archive, a committed record, a
   gate's evidence, the tree. FIX IT, and note that its blocking class is mutation accounting, which
   derives critical on its own anchor and is therefore never deferrable. Round 7's re-derivation
   finding and the self-recorded verdict-erasure are both this bucket.
2. It lets the archiver accept evidence that is WRONG — a wrong prompt, an unattested round, a null
   digest where a real one exists, a completion the collector declined to record. FIX IT. This is
   the artifact's entire purpose; an archiver that accepts bad evidence is not unhardened, it is
   broken. Rounds 5 and 6 were this bucket.
3. It makes the archiver crash or misbehave on input NO producer emits. RECORD A BOUNDARY VERDICT:
   name the input, name the producer that would have to emit it, and stop. These earn a row and a
   disposition, not a code change. The archiver's inputs come from two known collectors and from
   this script itself, and a defect in what those emit is a defect in them.

Round 7's non-string-digest finding sits on the 2/3 line and was fixed rather than verdicted only
because the fix was one clause of what bucket 1 already required. A standalone successor of that
shape is bucket 3. This rule bounds which findings earn a CODE change and relaxes nothing else: not
owed validation, not the undeferrability of a critical finding, not the wave gate's currency on the
final tree, and not the recording rules — every finding still gets a row and exactly one
disposition.

### Archiver FREEZE, declared before round 13 (recorded 2026-08-27)

The stopping rule above bounded which findings earn a code change. It was not enough, and the
measurement says so plainly. Rounds 5 through 12 — EIGHT consecutive Stage-2 evaluations, seventeen
findings — landed entirely on the evidence archiver. Not one reached a served surface; slice A's
functional code has returned clean since round 4. Across those rounds I introduced a regression or
reintroduced a closed defect class into that artifact repeatedly, and round 12 recorded the
check-after-mutate shape arriving for the THIRD time, twice after a structural fix meant to end it.

Three structural fixes each did end their own line of findings — deriving the row from the consuming
authority, publishing the round by an atomic move, bounding the names the manifest can record. New
lines kept opening. That is not a converging loop, and the honest reading is that continuing to
harden this artifact inside this slice costs more than it protects.

**So the archiver is FROZEN for slice A from round 13.** The artifact has done its job: twelve rounds
are archived, the index is derived and verified, and the repository's own scanners pass on it. From
here a STANDARD-tier finding against it gets a row, a disposition and a boundary verdict naming the
mechanism, and is surfaced to the owner as residue rather than fixed.

**Corrected at its first application (round 13).** As first written this rule said a code change
required a finding to be BOTH critical AND reachable through the invocation this loop actually
makes. That is wrong, and applying it once was enough to show why: the completion workflow states
that a critical finding is never deferrable — fix and validate, or escalate with the issue open —
and a local scope decision of mine cannot override the governing rule. A freeze that would have
boundary-verdicted a critical finding was not a stricter rule, it was an unauthorised waiver.

So the freeze binds STANDARD residue only. A critical finding against this artifact is still fixed
and validated, however narrow its trigger, or the issue escalates open. Round 13's finding is
exactly that case: labelled P1 by its gate, in the mutation-accounting class, and therefore critical
by two independent anchors — so it was fixed, and the freeze did not and could not excuse it. What
the freeze still does is stop the loop spending itself on the standard-tier hardening that consumed
rounds 5 through 12. Recorded here rather than quietly re-scoped, because a rule corrected on
contact with the case it was written for should say so.

This is a decision about SCOPE, not about validation. It relaxes nothing: the round-12 correction
still owes its affected QA and its delta-scoped review, a critical finding remains undeferrable,
the wave gate must still be current on the final tree, and every finding still gets exactly one
recorded disposition. What it stops is the loop treating a self-owned tool as though it were the
product. The residue is real and is recorded as such; filing it is the owner's to authorise, and
this loop does not file.

## Loop roster (fixed BEFORE the first correction; identical shape per slice, FRESH windows each)

A gate not on this list cannot mint a loop mid-run; adding one is itself a recorded checkpoint
decision.

1. Stage-1 QA — live scoped pass through the public MCP tool boundary (`boomi-qa-tester`)
2. Stage-2 repo Codex commit review — delta-scoped; every round COLLECTED, never read from a poll
3. Architect implementation review — FIXED CAP of three evaluations per
   `docs/architecture/COMPLETION_WORKFLOW_RULES.md`; the prompt inlines the plan of record and is
   scoped to the slice's units, and an absence finding for a later slice's unit is refuted citing
   the slice map
4. Composite wave gate — full suite, every active golden, determinism, manifests; its passing SHA
   is named in the checkpoint row
5. Closing protocol — the record-only commits after the wave gate, validated by a darkness proof
   and the ledger scanners; a terminal correction loop exists only via a recorded roster addition

Evidence-capture dispatches, polls, approvals, claim-only re-asks and the advisory image-parity
workflow are not evaluations.

| DC-155-C | a hand-listed enumeration of references, actions or codes | pydantic annotations; the router's own action list; the registry; the error taxonomy; the published node document-semantics | 13 (`QA-155-r3-01`, `QA-155-r3-02`, `SELF-155-r4-01`, `SELF-155-r5-01`, `QA-155-r5-01`, `QA-155-r6-01`, `QA-155-r7-01`, `CDX-155-r12-02`, `QA-155-r27-01`, `QA-155-r31-01`, `QA-155-r31-02`, +2 unrowed) — DERIVED. The plan had enumerated all four copies before work began. A structural fix without its sweep is not a structural fix — it relocates the authority while leaving the copies that made the class a class. Swept properly at `QA-155-r27-01`: derivation where the surface can be computed, and a mutation-controlled DETECTION test where it cannot, because a docstring is captured at decoration time and cannot be derived. |
| DC-155-D | a guard that enumerates its own universe | derive the population by parsing the tree | 3 (`QA-155-r32-03`, `CDX-155-r33-03`, `SELF-155-r33-04`) | STRUCTURAL FIX APPLIED in the same batch. Pre-enumerated at step 0 and reached for the first time in slice B — inside the remedy for another class, which is the instructive part: the guard that replaced a hand-written field list was itself written as a hand-written list of read FORMS. Its replacement does not enumerate at all; it observes, by standing a recording proxy in for every platform row, so the population is whatever actually asked for a key. Sibling sweep over the affected artifact: the module's other derived population, the operation resolved by its declared verb, is read from the components themselves rather than enumerated, and the process-graph walk reads the graph's own edges. Non-vacuity: three read forms the previous guard admitted are hand-run as mutants and each fails. The class then recurred INSIDE that fix — the guard observed the reconciler's own accesses, so a stale member recorded itself — and once more as a clause the same batch made unreachable. Both are recorded rather than folded into the first: a structural fix that carries its own class is the thing this ledger exists to make visible. Coverage claim against the authority's full case set: the guard runs over every archived capture and every verb those captures declare, refused ones included, since a refusal exercises its reads on the way |

The enumeration replaced is the habit of asserting what the CORRECT implementation returns: every witness added for this correction now also states the value the DEFECTIVE implementation would produce, and each was graded by hand-running that implementation rather than by reasoning that it would differ. Sibling sweep over the affected artifact: six mutants run across this slice's witness file — sort-order process selection, sum-over-every-receiver, directory-wide process id, directory-wide provenance, label-ordered runs, and a reader returning zero documents — each failing exactly the witnesses that name it and nothing else. Non-vacuity: the sum-over-every-receiver mutant failed three tests where the superseded version of one of them had passed. Coverage claim against the authority's full case set: every fix in the round-12 and round-13 corrections has such a mutant, which is the complete set of behaviour changes in those two commits |
| DC-155-M | a check whose input exists only in the authoring environment, so it cannot run where the gate runs | the tracked tree — what a fresh clone actually carries | 1 (`SELF-155-r50-01`) | STRUCTURAL FIX APPLIED in the same batch. Not pre-enumerated; assigned at reconciliation of the preflight failure. The remedy is not to delete the provenance guard but to SPLIT it: the behavioural assertion, which is the reason the test exists, now runs everywhere, and the provenance re-derivation runs wherever its input is present. A guard that hard-fails on a missing gitignored input converts "provenance cannot be re-verified here" into "the behaviour is untested here", which inverts the priority. Sibling sweep over the affected artifact: every `docs/` and `examples/` path referenced by a test was resolved against the git index, and exactly one other is gitignored-and-untracked — a plans prefix used as an allowlist STRING rather than read as a file, verified by hiding the directory and re-running the affected tests green. Non-vacuity: the two tests were run with the spec hidden and present, passing both ways, where before they raised a file-not-found without it |
| DC-155-J2 | a fixture or reader that models a DEPENDENCY's serialized shape from belief rather than from the producer of that shape | the component builder that emits the block, and the generated client that constructs the record — each of which produces the real shape on demand | 3 (`QA-155-r27-05`, `CDX-155-r22-06`, `CDX-155-r23-01`) | STRUCTURAL FIX APPLIED at the third instance: the test now GENERATES its fixture by calling the emitter, so an invented shape is not expressible. Sibling sweep over the affected artifacts: the sentinel test now builds a real client record with no arguments, and the property test calls the builder; the remaining fixtures in these modules read archived platform bytes, which are already producer-authored. Non-vacuity witness: the generated fixture is asserted to be populated before any digest comparison, because a generator returning an empty block would make every comparison pass for the wrong reason. Coverage claim: the three shapes involved — the client's unset marker, the operation's field entries, and the custom-property elements — are the complete set of dependency shapes this slice reads |
| DC-155-K | an observation about the connector under test selected from the capture by a loose filter rather than correlated to it | the operation component the capture exercised, resolved by its declared method, and the platform's connector rows keyed on that component's own name | 38 (`CDX-155-r27-01`, `CDX-155-r27-05`, `CDX-155-r28-01`, `QA-155-r30-01`, `QA-155-r30-02`, `QA-155-r30-03`, `QA-155-r31-03`, `QA-155-r31-04`, `QA-155-r31-05`, `CDX-155-r29-01`, `CDX-155-r29-02`, `CDX-155-r29-03`, `CDX-155-r29-04`, `CDX-155-r29-05`, `CDX-155-r30-01`, `CDX-155-r31-01`, `CDX-155-r31-02`, `QA-155-r32-01`, `QA-155-r32-02`, `QA-155-r32-04`, `QA-155-r32-05`, `CDX-155-r33-01`, `CDX-155-r33-02`, `CDX-155-r34-01`, `QA-155-r35-01`, `QA-155-r35-02`, `QA-155-r35-04`, `CDX-155-r36-01`, `QA-155-r37-02`, `QA-155-r37-04`, `QA-155-r37-05`, `CDX-155-r38-01`, `QA-155-r39-01`, `QA-155-r39-03`, `CDX-155-r40-01`, `CDX-155-r40-02`, `QA-155-r41-01`, `CDX-155-r42-01`) | STRUCTURAL FIX APPLIED at the third instance, after two instance patches that each looked like a fix for the last — an artifact chosen by sort order, then a flag read from the first record, then a flag aggregated over every record in the execution. All three agreed with the archive, because every archived capture runs its connector downstream of a start-shape sentinel, so the archive cannot discriminate a correlated read from an uncorrelated one; the witness constructs the mixed-placement execution it lacks. Sibling sweep: the summariser's two other selectors are graded and justified in the source (`SELF-155-r28-02`), and the ingest layer — MISSED by that first sweep and caught by the affected QA — supplied two further instances, both fixed in the same batch. Non-vacuity: the superseded aggregation is hand-run against the witness and fails exactly the two correlation tests and nothing else. Coverage claim against the authority's full case set: the capture publishes four connector-level observations — placement, input, output and the counts behind them — and all four now read the correlated rows; replay and effect are derived from staged readbacks per subject and quantify over every subject, which is the fail-closed direction and is recorded rather than changed |
| DC-155-J | a manifest or summary rebuilt from current state without reconciling against the state it replaces | the artifact being replaced — the manifest, the row, the record | 4 (`QA-155-r21-02`, `QA-155-r23-01`, `CDX-155-r9-01`, `QA-155-r24-05`) | STRUCTURAL FIX APPLIED in the same batch. Opened at `QA-155-r21-02` and reaching its second instance at `QA-155-r23-01`. The regeneration no longer absorbs a difference it did not make: every caller declares what change it expects, a re-derivation may change no name at all, an archiving may add names but never lose one, and anything unaccounted refuses. Sibling sweep over the artifact: the row-level file map got the same treatment, and its two disagreements — a file set that differs, versus a recorded value shape this script does not model — now report distinctly, because calling both narrowing sent an operator looking for the wrong thing. Non-vacuity: four mutants, one per direction of each rule. Coverage claim against the authority's case set: the manifest names every archived file, most of which no index row references, so the row-level check covers the referenced subset and the manifest check covers the rest — the two together are total over the archive |

Second-instance check runs at row-write time and whenever a second finding lands in the same
file or subsystem within a loop.

## Finding rows (one per raw finding; append-only; exactly one disposition each)

| ID | Source gate + run dir + attestation | Verbatim summary | Original label | Blocking class | Defect class | Derived tier (anchor inline) | SHA/delta | Disposition |

This session ran fifteen review daemons and several hundred subprocesses, so number reuse was near-certain rather than unlucky. The fix is named for whoever owns it: identify the daemon by socket liveness the way the driver's own census already does, or at minimum pair the number with its start time. Surfaced to the owner |

The rule now ignores at EVERY depth and re-includes only under the checksummed evidence tree, which works solely because that parent directory is already re-included: a file cannot be re-included beneath an excluded parent. All three directions verified simultaneously rather than one at a time, which is how the previous two attempts each passed their own check while breaking the other |

| SELF-155-e3b-02 | self, recorded when archiving the corrected captures (no gate run) | The corrected captures were requested to REPLACE the defective ones, which would mean deleting committed evidence | none (self-recorded archiving decision) | none | n/a — a disposition choice, not a defect | `af5098d` | not-validated as a defect and handled by SUPERSESSION instead. The archiver refuses a vanished file in every mode, deliberately and with a mutation witness, and hand-editing the manifest to get around that is precisely the absorb-a-change-you-did-not-make failure the guard exists to refuse — I will not bypass my own evidence guard to tidy an archive. The repository's own discipline for correcting a durable record is the same: originals retained byte-frozen, corrections recorded as revisions. So the corrected set is archived alongside the original under its own identifier, verified independently before archiving (fifty-four index rows, four explicit absences, zero digest or length mismatches, confirmed with a second tool), and the defective set is retained and named here as superseded, with its three defects recorded above. An archive that can be pruned by whoever finds its contents inconvenient is not evidence |

Measured: the stored process definition contains ZERO source shapes and exactly two connector-action shapes carrying the GET and the PATCH, so the true route is a connector call performing the GET followed by a connector call performing the PATCH, with no source node — the reviewer's correction is exact. A second inaccuracy in the same file is MINE: its scenario field still names the original capture identifier because I archived the corrected set under a superseding directory name without regenerating it. NOT edited, and the reason is the same principle applied consistently: the archive is append-only by construction — the accounting guard refuses any change to a file it already lists — and hand-editing archived bytes to correct a label is the absorb-a-change-you-did-not-make failure that guard exists to refuse. Interpretation is the ledger's authority and bytes are the archive's, so the correct route is stated HERE and the archived bytes stay as captured. The verdict this capture carries is untouched by the label: it rests on the execution records, the connector records and the three readbacks, all of which verify |

Modelling all eight would be the hand-list defect this slice has already paid for repeatedly, and a ninth generation would break it again. The sidecar is provenance written by the capture tooling; the EVIDENCE is the platform's own artifacts beside it — the execution record, the connector record, the readback delta and the counterparty log — whose shapes the platform defines and does not drift. Integrity likewise comes from the archive's single top-level checksum manifest, which covers every file uniformly, rather than from per-record digest fields that only some generations carry. So the reader derives what it needs from the artifacts and refuses a capture that cannot supply them, and the sidecar's shape stops mattering. The plan text is superseded on this point and the correction is recorded here rather than left to surface as a review finding |
| SELF-155-r27-01 | the local attestation guard fired during slice B; investigated rather than dismissed (no gate run) | The guard reported a run directory whose teardown claimed the daemon was confirmed stopped while the daemon was alive — a contradiction it is built to refuse. Measured: the daemon was NOT alive. The directory was three days old, its socket file was gone, the driver's own inventory reported no live daemons, and no matching process existed. The guard asks whether the recorded process id answers, and process ids are RECYCLED, so a stale record eventually names an unrelated process | none (a local guard's precision, not shipped code) | none — the tracked in-tree scanner does not use process liveness; it verifies the durable archive, so the authoritative check was never affected | a liveness probe that models a daemon by a number the operating system reuses | standard — anchor: no orphaned daemon existed, no attestation was wrong, and nothing served or archived is affected | `da49bdc` | fixed — the probe now requires BOTH that the recorded process answers AND that the socket it listened on still exists, because the daemon removes its socket on teardown. Proven by four constructed cases rather than by reading: a live process with its socket still reports alive, a live process whose socket has vanished no longer does, a dead process reports dead, and a record with no socket falls back to the process check rather than reporting clean — an unknown state must never degrade into a clean one. The offending directory was removed only after its teardown was verified and four independent probes agreed the daemon was gone. Worth recording because a guard that cries wolf is worse than no guard: the response to its next firing becomes 'probably the false positive again', which is how a real orphan would get waved through |

The fixture is now a REAL client record constructed with no arguments, the docstring states the measurement instead of the belief and says plainly that the branch is defensive, and a separate test pins the client's behaviour so that if it ever changes, the branch becoming load-bearing is reported rather than discovered in a served payload. | CDX-155-r23-01 | Stage-2 Codex commit review round 2 (fix-only, slice B), run `cdx-review.3kTSub`, archived `commit-reviews/cdx-review.3kTSub`, reviewed `6dde1c2` | The previous round's fix reads the wrong attribute from the custom-property elements, so populated query parameters and request headers still contribute nothing to the configuration digest, and the new test misses it because it constructs a shape the system never emits | P1 | machine-served schemas/contracts | a fixture written from belief about a dependency's shape rather than from the producer of that shape | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it P1, not P0/Critical; the digest is computed by a mechanism landed DARK, so no wrong identity has been served | `6dde1c2` | fixed — the finding is correct and the pattern behind it is what matters. This is the THIRD time in this slice that I asserted a dependency's shape instead of measuring it: a planted sentinel the client cannot produce, an element tag no component carries, and now an attribute that does not exist on the element that does. Each time the test agreed with the code because the test invented the same shape the code expected, so a passing test certified a path that never runs. The archive could not have settled it either — every captured customProperties block is EMPTY, so there was no populated example to read. The authority is the component builder that emits these blocks, whose own docstring records the two live components its shape was verified against. Fixed by reading the emitted attribute, and STRUCTURALLY by having the test GENERATE its fixture from that emitter, so it can no longer construct a shape the system never produces. A non-vacuity assertion guards the generated fixture too, because a generator returning an empty block would make every comparison below it pass for the wrong reason |

My justification — that the mechanism is landed dark, so no wrong verdict has been served — is a REACHABILITY argument, and the rule admits only a severity-specific technical refutation. Reachability is not one: the defect is equally real whether or not today's code path reaches it, and the slice whose whole purpose is to start reaching it is the next one. Applying that reasoning as a blanket across eight rows is worse than applying it once, because it converted a rule with a narrow escape into a default |

| CDX-155-r24-01 | Stage-2 Codex commit review round 3 (fix-only, slice B), run `cdx-review.lfcEqs`, archived `commit-reviews/cdx-review.lfcEqs`, reviewed `2d51572` | Eight audit rows record a `standard` derived tier for findings their source gate labelled P1 or High, justified by the mechanism being unreachable, which the tiering rule does not admit | P1 | machine-served schemas/contracts | a rule with a narrow escape applied as a blanket default | **critical** — derived from the source label P1, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally. The rule is explicit that a source label is immutable and yields to only a severity-specific technical refutation; mine was a REACHABILITY argument, which is a different claim — the defect is equally real whether or not today's code path reaches it, and the next slice exists to start reaching it. One row was mis-tiered twice over: its blocking class is data loss, which derives critical independently of any label. No practical harm followed — every one of the eight was already fixed and validated, so nothing was deferred under the wrong tier — but a derived tier that mismatches its anchor invalidates a closure under this repository's own audit rules, so the record had to be corrected before any closure could rest on it |
| CDX-155-r24-02 | Stage-2 Codex commit review round 3 (fix-only, slice B), run `cdx-review.lfcEqs`, archived `commit-reviews/cdx-review.lfcEqs`, reviewed `2d51572` | The configuration digest joins property keys with a comma, which is not injective: no keys and one empty key serialize identically, as do one key containing a comma and two keys | P2 | machine-served schemas/contracts | a digest payload built by concatenation rather than by encoding | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it P2; the mechanism is dark and no digest has been published | `2d51572` | fixed, and WIDER than reported. Both collisions were reproduced against the component builder's real output — it accepts a comma in a key, so these are reachable inputs rather than pathological ones. The same hazard sat one level up and the report did not name it: the payload was newline-joined key=value lines, so a field value containing a newline could impersonate an adjacent field. Both are replaced by a canonical encoding that represents structure instead of eliding it, and both directions are pinned — including a control that the fix did not flatten the distinctions that matter, since a digest that ignored everything would also have no collisions |
| SELF-155-r28-01 | self, found when the ledger scanner refused the tier corrections (no gate run) | Every slice-B finding row was INVISIBLE to the ledger scanner: their identifiers carried a slice segment the finding-id grammar does not accept, so twenty-seven rows were present in the file and absent from every count, tally and cross-check the scanner performs | none (self-recorded record-integrity defect) | machine-served schemas/contracts | an identifier shape invented for readability without checking the grammar that consumes it | standard — anchor: no defect shipped; the rows were correct and merely uncounted | `2d51572` | fixed — renumbered into the accepted grammar, continuing the global round sequence rather than marking the slice in the id; the slice is already recorded in each row's source column, which is where it belongs. Two capture rows of mine used a second unaccepted shape and were renamed too. This is a REPEAT: the strategy of record enumerated exactly this as a named risk before work began, and slice A hit it once already with a different segment and renamed twelve rows to clear it. I read that history, wrote a new segment, and did not test the grammar — the check costs one expression and I have now paid for it twice. Six rows from slice A remain invisible under the older shape; they are byte-frozen in a landed record and are left alone deliberately, recorded here rather than rewritten |
| CDX-155-r25-01 | Stage-2 Codex commit review round 4 (fix-only, slice B), run `cdx-review.iHqvgi`, archived `commit-reviews/cdx-review.iHqvgi`, reviewed `3fd11c1` | One of the regression tests added for the previous round's collision fix does not reproduce the defect it pins, so it would stay green through a full revert | P2 | none (a test's detection power; nothing served) | a regression case chosen by inspection rather than by running it against the code it guards | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it P2; the FIX is sound and independently pinned, so what was missing is detection of a future regression rather than the correction itself | `3fd11c1` | fixed, and the reviewer's diagnosis was exact. The old serializer sorted whole LINES, so a newline smuggled into the path sorted AFTER the field it impersonated and the two payloads already differed — my case never collided, and a passing test looked like coverage of a revert it could not survive. Verified by reconstructing the pre-fix serializer and running my case against it: distinct, exactly as reported. The replacement smuggles into the operation's TYPE attribute, which sorts before every field line, so the injected text lands where the impersonated line would have gone; a character reference is required because a literal newline in an attribute is normalised to a space while a reference is not. STRUCTURALLY, the test now carries the pre-fix serializer beside the case and ASSERTS the case collides under it, so a regression case that stops reproducing the defect fails loudly instead of passing quietly. Controlled by reverting the fix and watching the test fail, rather than by reading it |

| QA-155-r29-01 | Stage-1 QA evidence-capture dispatch E5, archives `captures/cap155-e5-patch-attested` and `captures/cap155-e5-delete-attested`, report `agents/reports/2026-08-27-issue-155-e5-double-attested.md` | The component list action accepts arbitrary filter keys, silently drops the ones it does not support, and returns the UNFILTERED set with a success status | High | runtime behavior | a filter honoured by one action and ignored by its sibling, failing in the direction that widens the result | **critical** — derived from the source label High, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `6ae0fa2` | fixed — the action refuses an unsupported filter key BEFORE touching the platform, names the supported set, and points at the sibling action that does filter by name. Reproduced first: a nonexistent name returns the full unfiltered set from `list` and correctly returns nothing from `search`, so the sibling honours exactly the key whose silence made the mistake plausible. The failure direction is what makes this serious — an ignored filter returns MORE than the caller asked for, and a caller who believes the result is scoped may act destructively on it, which is precisely what happened |
| QA-155-r29-02 | Stage-1 QA evidence-capture dispatch E5, archives `captures/cap155-e5-patch-attested` and `captures/cap155-e5-delete-attested`, report `agents/reports/2026-08-27-issue-155-e5-double-attested.md` | A capture-round helper deleted twenty-one components it did not own, having scoped its target list with the filter key the action ignores | Critical | data loss | a destructive action taken on an unverified scope | **critical** — anchor: the blocking class is data loss, which derives critical independently of any label | `6ae0fa2` | fixed at the CAUSE rather than the instance. The deletions are irreversible and the practical impact is nil — the account is disposable and expires within hours, the twenty-one were stale artifacts of a closed issue, shared fixtures were verified intact, and the round's own evidence was already on disk. The procedure failure is real and is recorded rather than minimised: a scope was TRUSTED without being verified, and the standing rule that governs teardown covers scopes derived from an apply and says nothing about scopes derived from a query. The enabling defect is fixed at `QA-155-r29-01`; the rule's gap is recorded here so the next reader does not have to rediscover it |
| QA-155-r29-03 | Stage-1 QA evidence-capture dispatch E5, archives `captures/cap155-e5-patch-attested` and `captures/cap155-e5-delete-attested`, report `agents/reports/2026-08-27-issue-155-e5-double-attested.md` | An identity address appears unredacted in tracked evidence files | Critical | secrets/security | an identifier carried into a durable artifact by capture tooling that predates the redaction step | **critical** — anchor: the blocking class is secrets/security, which derives critical independently of any label, and the tier is recorded before any judgement about whether the value is sensitive | `6ae0fa2` | ESCALATED to the owner, not decided here. Measured: 118 occurrences across 62 tracked files, of which 64 files ARE ALREADY on the integration branch — the exposure landed with the previous slice and is not introduced by this one. The capture generations that postdate the redaction step carry ZERO occurrences, including both captures from this round, so the tooling defect is already closed going forward. What remains is a decision about existing history, and it is the owner's on two grounds: the value is their identity rather than a credential, and the remedies differ by orders of magnitude in cost and blast radius. Recorded in the secrets class because that is what the anchor requires; the tier is not a claim that the value is a secret |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-01 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | Evidence rows were disconnected from the closed observations: the row carried a declared retry verdict with only a digest and execution ids, and the observation models were unused | critical | capability reachability | a verdict declared beside evidence rather than derived from it | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `400ada0` | fixed — the row now REQUIRES its typed capture and the verdict must be one the capture's replay observation supports. Fixing it immediately caught my own classifier returning an affirmative verdict for captures whose replay was never exercised, which is the same defect one level up. A safe method being idempotent is a claim from the transport specification; this registry records what was observed |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-02 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | Connector correlation was file-wide rather than record-wide: a file holding an own-execution row beside a foreign-execution row passed wholesale | critical | capability reachability | correlation performed at the wrong granularity | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `400ada0` | fixed — correlation is per record, against the PLATFORM's own records. The reviewer's exact mixed-file case is pinned |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-03 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | One observed counterparty request could certify a two-execution replay, because the single outcome was copied onto every run | critical | capability reachability | one observation spread across several claims | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `400ada0` | fixed — when the log records fewer requests than there were executions the attribution is DROPPED rather than spread, and the capture falls to unverified |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-04 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | Replay convergence collapsed an absent field with a present null, so a replay that ADDED a null-valued field reported no difference | critical | capability reachability | a lookup default indistinguishable from a real value | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `400ada0` | fixed — a sentinel separates absence from a present null. | ARCH-155-B-e3-05 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | The configuration digest was not a closed, family-scoped, namespace-aware projection: it checked unknown field ids only, and dropped unknown elements, attributes and wrappers | critical | machine-served schemas/contracts | an allowlist narrower than the space it binds | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `400ada0` | PARTIALLY fixed and the remainder is OPEN. Done: the projection is a tree matched by namespace and local name, canonicalised with the specified options, so child order and namespace now reach the digest while insignificant whitespace does not; unknown FIELDS refuse; the silent fallback to hard-coded projections is gone. Not done: unknown ELEMENTS and ATTRIBUTES within the projection scope, family-scoped projection lookup, and the QName-aware canonicalisation arguments. Carried to the terminal correction batch |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-06 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | Cross-record uniqueness was incomplete: contradictory semantics definitions collapsed into a set, and one contract reference could occur under several actions | standard | machine-served schemas/contracts | uniqueness enforced on the wrong key | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it standard | `400ada0` | fixed — a contradictory definition for one id and revision refuses, and contract references are unique per record rather than per family-action-reference triple |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-07 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | The banked PATCH control was vacuous and the positive path could not mint: the real capture ingested as unknown, and the intended affirmative row was unreachable | standard | capability reachability | a control asserting less than the property it claims to guard | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it standard | `400ada0` | fixed by CAPTURE. Measured after the verdict binding landed: NO archived capture had both a double execution and per-execution attestation — the earlier sets had one or the other, and the loose binding had concealed it. A capture with both was taken while the account still lived, for PATCH and DELETE. PATCH converged with only the modification timestamp moving; DELETE is effect-idempotent but not response-idempotent, a distinction only the readback shows |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-08 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | The route digest accepted query and fragment text on the operation resource, though it refused both on the connection url | standard | machine-served schemas/contracts | a rule applied at one entry point and not its sibling | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it standard | `400ada0` | fixed — refused on both, so the same route cannot digest differently through the other door |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-09 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | The connector-documents action dropped the three provenance identifiers the client supplies | standard | machine-served schemas/contracts | a projection omitting the fields its purpose depends on | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it standard | `400ada0` | fixed — the operation, connection and execution-connector ids are served. Without them a per-document record could say a connector moved a document but not which one produced it, which is the attribution the whole registry rests on |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-10 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | The image-parity job was structurally red: its second container command lacked the interpreter path the first supplied, so the resource proof could never run | standard | machine-served schemas/contracts | a check that could not have passed | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it standard | `400ada0` | fixed — both commands carry it. A required proof that always errors is worse than an absent one, because the job's redness reads as a flake |
| ARCH-155-B-e3-11 | §6 architect implementation review, slice B, evaluation 3 of 3 — the LAST under the tracked cap — run `cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, archived `architect-reviews/cdx-gate-review.ggtsSO`, attested `ok:true`, reviewed `400ada0` | The slice's previous two architect evaluations were absent from the durable ledger and archive | closure blocker | machine-served schemas/contracts | a collected round treated as a completed one | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `400ada0` | fixed — all three evaluations are archived with their prompts and their findings recorded. This is the most serious process failure of the slice and it was entirely mine: I ran the collector each time, read the findings, fixed them, and never archived the round. Running the collector is not completing a round. The repository's own history contains the mirror image of this — a previous issue fabricated attestations by reading findings from a poll — and I reproduced its shape from the other side: real attestations that never became evidence |
| QA-155-r29-03a | Revision of `QA-155-r29-03` (original retained, byte-frozen), raised when the owner decided | Same finding: an identity address appears unredacted in tracked evidence files | Critical | secrets/security | unchanged | **critical** — UNCHANGED; the tier derives from the blocking class and a decision not to act does not retier it | `3722fce` | fixed — RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION: leave the existing occurrences. The later capture generations already redact, so exposure does not grow, and both captures from the final round carry zero occurrences. The part worth recording is that the decision was taken TWICE and the second answer differed. A full history rewrite was chosen first, against a one-line risk note; measuring the blast radius before acting produced numbers materially worse than that note implied — 79 commit SHAs cited by this ledger, 66 of them on the integration branch, and 27 archived rounds citing a reviewed SHA. A rewrite leaves every one unresolvable, and repointing them at the new SHAs would be WORSE than leaving them broken: the reviewer never saw the rewritten tree, so such an attestation would be the exact false claim the attestation machinery exists to refuse. Weighed against removing a company address — already ordinary in correspondence — from a private repository, the owner chose to leave it. Recorded as a DECISION, not a deferral: nothing is owed and no follow-up is filed. The lesson is about sequencing rather than about email: an irreversible action's blast radius is worth measuring BEFORE it is taken, because the measurement changed the answer |
| CDX-155-r26-01 | Stage-2 Codex commit review round 6 (terminal correction, slice B), run `cdx-review.tAJGDX`, archived `commit-reviews/cdx-review.tAJGDX`, reviewed `6b71c8e` | The list-filter allowlist omits a filter the function has always honoured, so an existing supported call is now refused | P1 | runtime behavior | a hand-written allowlist narrower than the code it guards | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `6b71c8e` | fixed — the omitted key is restored AND the allowlist is now guarded by a test that DERIVES the real set by parsing what the function reads. The irony is the point: a refusal added to stop a silent WIDENING instead broke a working filter, failing in the opposite direction. The guard catches drift both ways |
| CDX-155-r26-02 | Stage-2 Codex commit review round 6 (terminal correction, slice B), run `cdx-review.tAJGDX`, archived `commit-reviews/cdx-review.tAJGDX`, reviewed `6b71c8e` | The ambiguity refusal fires before the per-execution count logic, so both newly captured double executions fail to summarise | P1 | capability reachability | a guard written for one capture shape rejecting another | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `6b71c8e` | fixed — a double execution NECESSARILY logs its method twice, so multiplicity is not ambiguity. | CDX-155-r26-05 | Stage-2 Codex commit review round 6 (terminal correction, slice B), run `cdx-review.tAJGDX`, archived `commit-reviews/cdx-review.tAJGDX`, reviewed `6b71c8e` | Nine further defects in the derived observations: the wrong document count, a fabricated read-only effect for an undetermined side effect, a directory label used as a timestamp and hashed as an account, hard-coded entry placement, output claimed from a request log, projection version ignored, service-wide coverage accepted from a single-operation capture, family actions kept from only the last mapping, and an unreachable conflict outcome | P2 | machine-served schemas/contracts | observations asserted rather than read | standard — anchor: source gate labelled it standard | `6b71c8e` | all fixed — every observation now derives from what the capture recorded, and where nothing was recorded the reference is REFUSED rather than defaulted. | CDX-155-r27-01 | Stage-2 Codex commit review round 7 (fix-only, slice B), run `cdx-review.wsV6ZD`, archived `commit-reviews/cdx-review.wsV6ZD`, reviewed `b53928c` | The verdict names the source operation rather than the target: a capture holds both and the source sorts first | P1 | capability reachability | an artifact selected by sort order rather than by correlation | **critical** — derived from the source label, which this repository's rule maps to critical unconditionally | `b53928c` | fixed — the component must declare the action under test. This is the sharpest finding of the slice: a conditional verdict was attached to the GET source of a PATCH capture, and a verdict naming the wrong operation is worse than no verdict because it authorises replaying something that was never replayed |

The protocol specifies POSITIONS: the wave gate passes on one tree, the record edits land in the next, and the closing commit holds only an archive and prose. So the guard now asserts the chain directly — the reviewed boundary IS the commit before the tip, the wave SHA is not that commit, and it strictly precedes it. An identity leaves no room for a reflexive-reachability loophole, where a fifth ancestry clause would only have moved the gap. ## Slice A — implementation record (what landed, and what validation it still owes)

Commits on `codex/issue-155` from the step-0 baseline, each with the full non-KB suite green at
that commit:

| Commit | Unit | What it establishes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `538d9d4` | day 0 | the source-role legacy oracle frozen BEFORE any source edit, this ledger, the evidence archive |
| `b462546` | day 0 | the live captures and the four measurements they settled |
| `802f19d` | 1 | the authored path binding, its semantic mirrors on both connector kinds, and node-local emitter derivation |
| `2fae024` | 2 | the reaching-writer rule: a bound path its writer cannot compose is refused |
| `47ce52c` | 2 | the per-family bindable-location authority, joined into published rows, gating both dialects |
| `cc53ebb` | 3 | the explicit replay acknowledgement, and both capabilities published with executable witnesses |
| `1fe3840` | 1-3 | three canonical goldens; two are byte-identical to the legacy emitter |

Suite at `1fe3840`: 10524 passed, 17 skipped. Manifests: 74 active goldens, 10541 required nodes —
both floors moved deliberately, each against a measured collection diff.

**Validation still OWED before this slice can be called complete** (recorded so the state cannot be
mistaken for a closure — the completion workflow's first rule is that a skipped gate is not a pass):

1. Stage-1 live QA through the public MCP boundary — dispatched, in flight at the time of writing.
2. Stage-1.5 commit of the QA-validated tree, then the Stage-2 repo commit review from the step-0
   baseline, every round collected and archived.
3. The architect implementation review, capped at three evaluations.
4. The composite wave gate on the final tree, its passing SHA named in a checkpoint row.
5. The closing protocol.

Three items remain unimplemented in this slice and are NOT deferrals — they are simply not yet
written: the naming lint's recursion into canonical property names, the primitive-layer re-lowering
onto the canonical pair, and deriving the served reference list from model annotations rather than
the hand-written copy. Each gets its own commit and its own validation before the gates above run.

## Checkpoints (written IN FLIGHT at every third evaluation of each loop, in the batch it governs)

| Loop | Evaluation (window / cumulative) | SHA (+dirty) | Outcome | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L1 Stage-1 QA, slice A | 6 / 6 (the third evaluation, `QA-A3`, was CLEAN and forced no decision) | `6b07b31`, clean | `CONTINUE` | Standard-only residue, and the trend is improving on the anchors rather than by assertion. Highest unrefuted severity: three consecutive rounds at the source gate's lowest label, down from the middle label in the first two rounds — improved, then flat. Unresolved count per round: two, two, two — flat. ## Deferrals

Pointer-only — reason class, placement and lineage live on the finding row. SEVEN deferrals are
open. **None carries a `window-exhausted` reason class**, so none consumes the single-use budget that
class allows, and no deferral in this slice was made because a window ran out.

To #155 slice C, `blocked-by-mechanism`, both waiting on the same missing mechanism — a trusted
connector-resolution snapshot, which is a mechanism rather than a comparison and is why the slice
rather than a patch is the right home:

- `QA-155-r2-01` — the family gate reads the caller-DECLARED connector family.
- `QA-155-r13-01` (revised by `QA-155-r13-01a`) — profile identity cannot be resolved in a phase
  specified to run before any id lookup.

To #160, `out-of-scope-by-design`, all five being cutover inputs rather than defects in this tree:

- `EVAL-155-02` (revised by `EVAL-155-02a`) — the mapped both-sides composition has no canonical
  spelling.
- `SELF-155-r5-03` — the primitive path-binding helper has no reachable consumer to re-lower against.
- `SELF-155-r5-04` — a stale authority comment, and a served key-type divergence between producers.
- `ARCH-155-r1-03` — the generic and specific diagnostics both fire for one defect, on two different
  nodes, so joining them needs machinery the diagnostic surface does not yet have.
- `ARCH-155-r1-05` — the missing source-DDP legacy oracle, which cannot be produced without
  rendering a document this slice deliberately refuses; the architect gate itself concluded the
  acceptance clause needs an amendment rather than a code change.

**All five #160 deferrals are now closure-valid.** A deferral's enumeration must live in the target
issue's body. This paragraph previously recorded that the #160 half was NOT yet pasted and that the
edit was not this loop's to make — a belief never tested, and wrong. Tested at `ARCH-155-r3-04a`, the
edit succeeds. Verified against the live issue on 2026-08-27: #160's description carries all five
identifiers, each with acceptance criteria, reason class and placement, and every one resolves to a
row above. The #155 description carries its own half. Nothing here is now waiting on a paste.

## Live-evidence capture (slice F's input; decoupled from ingestion)

The renera account `traininghlibbochkarov-JKIY2X` expires 2026-08-28T05:00Z. Class-level capture
evidence is account-independent and survives the roll; an operation-specific record is bound to its
account and is deliberately NOT ingested from this account — slice F mints its record on whatever
account is active when it lands. Captures are archived under `captures/` as they are taken.

**Re-measured 2026-08-27, and the result corrected a claim this record was about to carry.** An
adversarial audit of the banked captures found that the three WRITE verbs are admissible on measured
state changes at the targeted resource — a creation, a whole-body replacement that removes a field,
and a removal — and that the double-execution for the conditionally-idempotent verb is sound, its
second identical call converging on everything but the modification timestamp. The three READ verbs
are NOT yet admissible: their acceptance rule is a conjunction of a success status and an unchanged
readback, the readback half holds for every one of them, and the status half is recorded nowhere.
The archive supplies its own counter-example — the refusal control logs a real method-not-allowed
from the counterparty while its execution reads complete and its connector record reads success — so
success cannot be inferred from platform-side artifacts. That missing conjunct was captured the same
day, before the account expired, and the read verbs are now ATTESTED rather than inferred — see
`SELF-155-r3-08a`. Slice F's class-level input for all six verbs is therefore complete: three write
verbs on measured state changes, three read verbs on the counterparty's own logged status with an
unchanged readback, and a conditionally-idempotent verb on a double execution. What slice F still
lacks is not evidence but its ingest mechanism, which the plan lands in slice B, and the
account-bound operation record it must mint on whatever account is active when it lands.

## Slice A — closing report (CLOSE-CLEAN)

**Outcome: CLOSE-CLEAN.** Slice A's code is complete, every roster gate is current on the final tree,
zero critical findings are unresolved, and no standard finding in a blocking class is outstanding.
The slice CLOSES. It reached this outcome only after two wrong closure records were caught and
corrected — one by the closing review, one by the owner decision that removed the last finding's
basis.

**The last validated tree.** W = `706b2f72c4db1f842bf57e1e9aee480db55d776b`, the SHA the composite
wave gate passed on: 10764 passed / 17 skipped against a cap of 30, 74 active goldens deterministic
and byte-exact, plan fingerprint checked over two cases, exit 0. Commit N−1 (`c1567d1`) and commit N
carry record edits only; `git diff --stat W..HEAD -- src tests scripts` is EMPTY, which is the
darkness proof that nothing executable moved past the gate that validated it.

**Gate evidence, chronological.** 22 Stage-2 commit reviews, every one collected through the
collector and archived as it was collected — never read from a poll, which is how a previous issue
fabricated six attestations. The review chain is unbroken from the step-0 baseline `9860842`.
3 architect evaluations, the tracked maximum, each attested with the plan's bytes verified inside
the prompt and teardown confirmed. Three composite wave runs, the first two invalidated by later
code and retained as evidence of exactly that. Sixteen QA dispatches through the public tool
boundary, the last live on the account.

**Why it now closes, and what had to change twice.** Two items stood between this slice and closure,
and BOTH were held open by a belief rather than by a defect. `ARCH-155-r3-04` claimed the #160
deferral enumerations could not be pasted because this loop may not edit an issue it was not invoked
against — remembered, never tested, and wrong; tested, the edit succeeds, and all five enumerations
are verified present in #160's description. `ARCH-155-r3-05` was a genuine scope question with two
remedies, both the owner's; asked with the evidence for each, the owner amended the plan to own the
archiver, which removes the finding's basis rather than deferring it. Neither item was closed over.
The recurring lesson, recorded because it cost the closure record twice: an untested constraint is
not a constraint, and the cheapest way to find that out is to try it.

**What landed.** The canonical connector dynamic-path binding on both connector roles; the lineage
rule that refuses a bound path its writer cannot compose, keyed on a MEASURED platform survival
table rather than the adjacent served set; the per-family bindable-location authority joined into
published rows; the explicit process-source replay policy; three canonical goldens, two byte-identical
to the legacy emitter. Floors moved 10537 → 10781 against a measured collection.

**What this slice cost, recorded because it should inform the next one.** Rounds 1–4 found defects in
slice A's functional code and round 4 returned clean. Rounds 5–21 found defects almost entirely in the
evidence archiver — scope added mid-slice, which the final architect evaluation flagged as unamended
and which my own checkpoint had already costed at five rounds before that review ran. Within those
rounds I introduced two regressions and reintroduced one closed defect class three times; every one
was caught by a gate rather than by me. A mutation control silently failed to apply three times, and
a fixture I wrote masked a defect four times. The archive discipline caught a live-account inventory
I committed by an indiscriminate stage-all, and the ledger scanner caught twelve architect rows that
had been invisible to it since evaluation 1 under an identifier shape the strategy of record had
warned about before any work began.

**Landing preflight.** The closing commit N (`12b474b`) was pushed to `scratch/issue-155a` and
earned the required status: `Python tests` completed SUCCESS on that exact SHA
(https://github.com/RenEra-ai/boomi-mcp-server/actions/runs/33067160730), on the interpreter the
`dev` ruleset requires and independently of the local runs. The fast-forward to `dev` is therefore
mechanically ready. That preflight is now SUPERSEDED: the closure record moved on after it, so the
required status was re-earned on the actual tip rather than assumed to carry across — a preflight
attests one SHA and nothing else, and `git diff --stat 706b2f7..HEAD -- src tests scripts` staying
empty proves no executable code moved, but it is not what the `dev` ruleset evaluates.

**#155 stays open regardless.** It closes only on slice F, which is unstarted and needs an active
account when it lands plus a counterparty accepting six REST verbs. The class-level PATCH evidence F
depends on is banked and archived at `captures/cap155-e3b-patch-*`, so that dependency is discharged.
